# Uchū Gijutsu v3.1 — Calibration Workflow for the Placeholder Parameters This document **supersedes** `Uchu_Gijutsu_v3_validated.md`: the core files `config.py`, `memory.py`, `orbit.py` and `radiation.py` changed (calibration hooks), so the complete, current tree is embedded below, verbatim. ## 0. Summary The v3 simulator had 60 parameters the physics depends on that were placeholders (cross-sections, spectra, orbit scalings, MBU/SEP statistics, power, thermal, performance). This release adds a workflow that **replaces placeholders with fitted, uncertainty-quantified values from measurements, tracks which ones are still guesses, and pushes the remaining uncertainty through the simulator.** | | | |---|---| | New code | `uchu/calibration/` — loaders, registry/audit, radiation fits, hardware fits, orchestrator + CLI, synthetic campaigns | | Tests | **237** (was 129), **98 %** line coverage, all passing | | Mutation check | **46/46** injected defects caught (15 original + 31 calibration) | | Validation | hidden-truth recovery on synthetic campaigns: **547/580 (94.3 %)** of 95 % intervals contain the truth over 20 independent campaigns | | Effect on outputs | SRAM upset-rate error **−68.5 % → −0.6 %**; mission power error **−25.7 % → −0.8 %**; mean temperature error **−21.3 K → −0.3 K** | > **Read this first.** Everything above is validated on **synthetic** measurements generated from a hidden truth *by the same model forms the workflow fits* (Weibull cross-sections, lumped thermal node, roofline). That proves the estimators, uncertainty quantification, identifiability guards and bookkeeping work. It does **not** prove those model forms describe your hardware. Real beam, bench and flight data will contain effects absent here (dosimetry error, SEL/SEFI contamination, temperature dependence, model-form error). The workflow detects some of these (over-dispersion, inconsistent flight counts, ill-conditioned designs) and says so; it cannot detect all of them. §7 lists what remains. ## 1. How the workflow works ``` measurements ─► validated loaders ─► per-family fits ─► joint epistemic draws ─► flight-data reweighting ─► calibrated config (CSV/JSON)      (reject bad data      (MLE / NLS +        (one draw = one          (importance weights,        + report (md/json)                   with row/col msgs)    bootstrap / Wald)    plausible chip+env)      consistency p-value)        + provenance ledger                                               │                                                                      │                                               └──── every fitted value written to cfg.calibration ──► placeholder_audit() ◄┘ ``` Design rules (each enforced by tests): 1. **Steps are isolated.** Bad or insufficient data in one step records a `failed` step and leaves *those* parameters unresolved and unchanged; it never aborts other steps, never substitutes a guess. Internal exceptions are reported as `INTERNAL …`, never disguised as data problems. 2. **Non-identifiable designs are refused, not fitted.** Condition numbers are judged on parameter-range-scaled Jacobians; a parameter with no sensitivity at the optimum is dropped and reported unresolved while the others still fit. 3. **Provenance is a ledger, not a comment.** `cfg.calibration[path]` holds source, method, value, 95 % interval, status (`measured | derived | design_fact | absorbed | accepted_placeholder`) and notes; it is part of the config hash. 4. **Uncertainty is propagated, not tabulated.** The report carries joint parameter draws; `sample_configs()` and `epistemic_mission_study()` run them through the simulator. 5. **Intervals that rest on placeholders say so.** Each region's rate summary reports the fraction of its rate carried by cross-section families still unresolved (§4). ### What resolves each placeholder | Placeholder family | Data required | Method | Uncertainty | Guards | |---|---|---|---|---| | Environment spectra, orbit scalings, shielding | orbit-specific integral-flux tables (CREME96 / OMERE / SPENVIS / AP-9) | installed directly (`environment_mode = "tabulated_orbit"`); **all** placeholder scalings bypassed | external tool's | table-coverage warning if a table starts above a cross-section threshold | | Cross-sections σ_sat, x_th, W, s (per region × ion/proton) | beam counts: LET or energy, fluence, events, units | Poisson maximum likelihood (Weibull), multi-start | parametric bootstrap (negative-binomial resample when over-dispersed) | deviance GOF p-value, dispersion, bound / saturation / degeneracy warnings; zero-event data → upper limit only | | MBU probability, cluster-size law, max cluster | strike multiplicity histogram | closed-form MLE (binomial + truncated geometric) | Wilson; Jeffreys draws | χ² GOF; truncation warning | | SEP rate, duration, intensity law | event catalogue + observation span | exponential / lognormal / Poisson MLE | Garwood, χ², t | <3 events refused, <10 warned | | `avf_sram` | none (computed) | real bit-flip injection into GEMMs | Wilson; **upper bound stored** (conservative) | — | | Power: base, static fraction, leak slope, clock share | bench sweep over T, f, V, util | nonlinear least squares, relative residuals | Wald → MVN draws | range-scaled condition number; at-bound warnings | | Thermal: C, radiator area, Earth/Sun loads | telemetry with eclipse flags and varying power | Newton/backward-Euler trajectory fit (verified equal to `LumpedThermalModel`) | Wald → MVN draws | refused if C not identifiable; missing eclipse/sunlit leaves that load unresolved | | Performance: `pipeline_overlap`, `hbm_efficiency`, `sparse_speedup_at_50` | GEMM latencies (+ launch overhead nuisance) | log-latency least squares | Wald → MVN draws | per-parameter regime counts; zero-sensitivity drop; `design_coverage()` *before* benchmarking | | On-orbit EDAC counts | counts, exposure windows, SEP catalogue | importance reweighting of the beam-derived draws; optional lognormal model-discrepancy | posterior over draws | prior-predictive consistency p-value, ESS | | **Not fittable from these data:** `avf_register/logic`, `gates_per_pe`, `reg_bits_per_pe`, `set_pulse_width_ps`, `tmr_residual`, `sel_recovery_s`, `interconnect_frac`, `interconnect_extra_exponent` | netlist / RTL fault injection / layout / ops decisions | `declare(cfg, path, value, source)` | — | stay **unresolved** in the audit until declared | ## 2. Quick start ```bash pip install numpy scipy pytest pytest-cov python tools/calibration_demo.py            # synthetic campaign -> CSV files -> plan.json -> CLI -> report python -m uchu.calibration plan.json --out out/     # exit 0 ok · 2 a step failed · 3 plan/file error ``` ```python from uchu import SimConfig, build_transformer_workload from uchu.calibration import (load_plan, run_calibration, CalibrationOptions, placeholder_audit, require_calibrated,                               declare, epistemic_mission_study) cfg, data, opts = load_plan("plan.json")                  # or build CalibrationData in Python rep = run_calibration(cfg, data, CalibrationOptions(seed=1, n_boot=200, n_draws=200)) rep.save("out/")                                          # calibrated_config.json, calibration_report.{json,md} print(rep.audit.render())                                 # what is STILL a guess declare(rep.config, "radiation.avf_register", 0.35, "RTL fault injection run 14")   # known facts get recorded, too require_calibrated(rep.config, ["radiation.sram.ion.sigma_sat_cm2", "radiation.sram.proton.sigma_sat_cm2"])   # gate a study study = epistemic_mission_study(rep, build_transformer_workload(num_layers=12), mission_s=86400, n=32) print(study["sdc_per_hr"], study["T_max_k"])              # spread of outputs caused by calibration uncertainty ``` `plan.json` (paths relative to the plan; see `write_campaign` in `synthetic.py` for a complete generated example): ```json {"base_config": "start_config.json", "options": {"seed": 7, "n_boot": 200, "n_draws": 200}, "environment": {"source": "CREME96 run A, 550 km 51.6°, 2 mm Al", "tables": {"gcr_ion": "gcr.csv", "trapped_proton": "trapped.csv",                  "sep_ion": "sep_ion.csv", "sep_proton": "sep_p.csv"}}, "beam":  [{"file": "sram_ion.csv", "region": "sram", "kind": "ion"}, {"file": "sram_proton.csv", "region": "sram", "kind": "proton"}], "mbu": {"file": "mbu.csv"}, "sep": {"file": "sep.csv", "obs_span_s": 788940000}, "power": {"file": "power.csv"}, "thermal": {"file": "thermal.csv"}, "perf": {"file": "perf.csv"}, "flight": [{"name": "unit A", "region": "sram", "n_units": 5.4e8, "segments": [[0, 3e7], [3.5e7, 6.3e7]], "events": 319,              "flare_events": [[1.2e7, 1.3e7, 0.8]]}], "avf": [{"field": "avf_sram", "dtype": "int8", "tol": 0.01, "trials_per_bit": 100}], "design_facts": [{"path": "radiation.gates_per_pe", "value": 1800, "source": "netlist rev C"}]} ``` CSV schemas (`#` comments and blank lines ignored; every error names file, row and column): | file | columns | |---|---| | beam, ion | `let_mev_cm2_mg, fluence_cm2, events` + optional `tilt_deg, n_units` — LET→LET/cosθ, fluence→F·cosθ (≤ 60°) | | beam, proton | `energy_mev, fluence_cm2, events` + optional `n_units` | | spectrum | `x, phi_integral` — Φ(>x) in particles/cm²/s, x ascending, Φ non-increasing | | mbu | `cluster_size, count` | | sep | `duration_s, intensity_rel` (relative to the reference event in the SEP tables) + `obs_span_s` in the plan | | power | `T_k, f_ghz, v_v, util, power_w` (junction temperature) | | thermal | `t_s, power_w, eclipse, T_k` — row *i* describes the interval **ending** at `t_s`; row 0 is the initial condition | | perf | `M, K, N, dtype, sparsity, freq_ghz, latency_s` | **Experiment design helpers** (run them *before* spending bench time): `suggest_power_design()` (18 points, condition ≈ 10²), `suggest_gemm_shapes(cfg)` and `design_coverage(cfg)` (how many shapes are informative per parameter **on your chip**: e.g. on the default 2 TB/s chip no GEMM is ever bandwidth-limited, so `hbm_efficiency` is unobservable and `design_coverage` says 0). ## 3. Interpreting the output | message | meaning | what to do | |---|---|---| | `over-dispersed (deviance/dof = …)` | Poisson noise does not explain the scatter | check fluence/dosimetry, SEL/SEFI contamination; intervals were widened but the point estimate is suspect | | `estimate sits on a search bound for [...]` | parameter not determined by the data | rely on the *predicted rate* interval, not on that parameter (see §5) | | `saturation not reached …` | σ_sat is an extrapolation | add higher-LET points | | `⚠ N % rests on placeholder … cross-section` | that region's rate interval excludes the uncertainty of an unresolved cross-section | calibrate it, or treat the interval as meaningless | | `flight count … inconsistent with the beam-derived model` | prior-predictive p < 0.01 | environment/shielding/geometry wrong, or a mechanism absent from beam data; use `flight_discrepancy_log_sigma` only after investigating | | `ill-conditioned design` / `cannot identify` | measurements do not vary the parameters independently | add the measurements named in the message | | `zero sensitivity at the optimum` | that regime does not bind on this chip | parameter stays a placeholder (unresolved) | | `absorbed` (ledger) | confounded with another fitted parameter (e.g. ECC overhead into `hbm_efficiency`) | nothing; recorded so it is not mistaken for measured | ## 4. Validation ### 5.1 Hidden-truth recovery (seed 11, `n_boot = 100`) A truth config differing from every default placeholder family generated measurements; the analyst's start config had default 0.2195 | [0.2177, 0.2217] | yes | Note the cross-section rows: `x_th`, `width` and `shape` are individually weakly determined (intervals span factors of 3–15, `x_th` often sits on its search bound — the fit says so), yet the **predicted rate**, which is what the simulator uses, is tight (next table). This is the usual Weibull parameter degeneracy; propagate the rate, do not interpret single Weibull parameters. ### 5.2 Interval coverage over 20 independent campaigns (`n_boot = 100`) Nominal level 95 %. Measured fraction of intervals containing the truth: | family | contained | fraction | |---|---|---| | MBU | 39/40 | 97.5 % | | SEP | 78/80 | 97.5 % | | power | 77/80 | 96.3 % | | thermal | 76/80 | 95.0 % | | performance | 57/60 | 95.0 % | | cross-section (beam) | 220/240 | **91.7 %** (percentile parametric bootstrap under-covers slightly at these counts; σ_sat alone: 88 % over 40 repeats) | | **all** | **547/580** | **94.3 %** | | predicted SRAM upset rate (95 % interval) | 18/20 | 90 % — median error −0.6 %, 90 % of campaigns within ±6.8 %; **the shipped placeholder was −68.5 % off** | The demo campaign above (seed 2024) happens to land at 25/29; its four misses are 2.1–2.6 standard errors (three are the correlated power parameters `static_frac`, `leak_temp_coeff`, `clock_frac` from a single 18-point noise realisation; one is `sep_intensity_median`). No parameter in either run misses by more than 2.6 s.e. Goodness-of-fit p-values for the Weibull fit are well calibrated (40 repeats: mean 0.48, 7 % below 0.05). Closed-form and conjugate checks: MBU interval coverage ≥ 92 %, SEP Garwood/χ² coverage ≥ 92 %, Jeffreys Gamma coverage ≥ 93 %, flight importance-weighting reproduces the exact Poisson–Gamma posterior (median within 2 %, interval within 5 %). ### 5.3 What calibration does to simulator outputs 3000 s LEO mission, 4-layer INT8 transformer (`run_mission`, same seed for all three): | config | avg power | dense TFLOPS | mean T | max T | |---|---|---|---|---| | truth | 40.53 W | 87.76 | 284.92 K | 295.74 K | | **calibrated** | **40.22 W (-0.8 %)** | **88.18 (+0.5 %)** | **284.59 K (-0.33)** | 295.34 K | | shipped placeholders | 30.11 W (-25.7 %) | 101.66 (+15.8 %) | 263.66 K (-21.26) | 290.00 K | Orbit-static SRAM upset rate (per bit per day): truth 8.534e-10; placeholder 2.685e-10; calibrated 8.432e-10 [7.867e-10, 9.098e-10]; after the flight log 8.336e-10 [7.868e-10, 8.948e-10] (flight: 319 observed vs 323 expected, consistency p = 0.89). Flight data tighten the interval by ≈ 30 %. ### 5.4 Test suite and mutation check 237 tests: `test_cal_data` 34 · `test_cal_radiation` 31 · `test_cal_hardware` 22 · `test_cal_workflow` 21 · `test_radiation` 38 · `test_simulator` 20 · `test_memory` 18 · `test_thermal` 16 · `test_rng_config` 16 · `test_orbit` 13 · `test_faults` 8. Eleven are marked `slow` (repeated-campaign statistics, ≈ 75 s total; whole suite ≈ 3 min). Line coverage 98 % (the 3-line `__main__.py` is exercised through a subprocess test that coverage does not trace). `python tools/mutation_check.py` re-introduces 46 defects into a scratch copy and requires the relevant non-slow tests to fail: **46/46 killed** — the 15 original v2 defects plus 31 calibration defects (NLL sign, missing deviance factor, bootstrap that does not resample, un-widened over-dispersed intervals, inverted MBU/SEP/flight estimators, cos-vs-1/cos LET, tilt not applied to fluence, power static/dynamic swap, wrong eclipse-interval convention, disabled identifiability check, config mutated in place, step failures reported as ok, …). ## 6. Defects found while building and validating this (kept, because they change how to trust the results) 1. **A bound I set was silently biasing a fit.** The performance fit capped launch overhead at 0.9 × the fastest measurement; the true value exceeded it, the parameter pinned at its bound and the other three compensated. Symptom: noise-free data recovered overlap 0.587 instead of 0.600 and residual rms 0.034 against 0.02 injected noise. Fixed (bound 0.999×); nuisance parameters at bounds are now flagged; a mutant re-introducing it is killed. *My earlier "truth inside the interval" results for this fit were partly luck.* 2. **Zero Jacobian from a staircase.** `ceil()` in the cycle count made the sparse-speedup derivative exactly zero at finite-difference scale; the parameter looked non-identifiable. Fits now use a smooth cycle count (`estimate_layer(..., exact_cycles=False)`, < 1 cycle per tile difference); the simulator keeps the exact count. 3. **Unit-dependent identifiability.** A column in seconds (launch overhead) dominated the condition number and made other parameters look weak. Conditioning and weak-parameter detection now use range-scaled Jacobians (condition 3×10⁵ → 5 for the same data). 4. **A fatal error where a graceful one was right.** A non-binding regime produced a zero-sensitivity column and aborted the whole fit. Now it is dropped and reported unresolved while the rest fit. The *test* I first wrote for this exercised a different code path; the mutation check exposed the survivor and I added the missing scenario. 5. **Misleading narrow interval.** The register rate showed [2.687, 2.689]×10⁻¹⁰ because its dominant proton cross-section was an unresolved placeholder. Reports now state the share of each rate resting on placeholders. 6. **Weak warnings.** The "saturation not reached" test missed data stopping at 71 % of σ_sat; unphysical thermal loads (projected-area fraction 1.5) produced no warning. 7. **Vacuous tests.** `pytest.approx` has a default absolute tolerance of 10⁻¹²; every comparison of ≈10⁻¹⁵ rates was trivially true. All rate comparisons now use `abs=0`. Two of my physical premises were then shown wrong by real failures: in the placeholder LEO model the SRAM rate is ≈ 10⁴× dominated by trapped protons, so solar activity and inclination barely move it. 8. **Statistically flawed single-seed assertions.** Asserting four 95 % intervals contain the truth in one seed fails ≈ 19 % of the time by construction. Single-seed tests now check |estimate − truth| ≤ 3.5 s.e.; nominal coverage is tested over repeated campaigns. 9. **Config hash instability** (`1` vs `1.0` in a table) — canonicalised to floats. Consequence worth knowing: **in LEO the proton cross-section calibration dominates the SRAM rate**; ion (LET) beam data mostly matter for GEO/deep space and for SEP events. Plan beam time accordingly. ## 7. Limitations — what this does not do - **Synthetic validation only** (see the note at the top). Model-form error is untested: if real devices deviate from Weibull σ(x), a lumped thermal node, or the roofline, the fits will still converge and report intervals that are too narrow. Over-dispersion, the GOF p-value, residual rms and the flight-consistency check are the only guards; consider `flight_discrepancy_log_sigma` > 0 once you understand the mismatch. - **Beam-fit intervals under-cover slightly** (91.7 %), and the Wald/MVN intervals for power/thermal/performance assume near-linear behaviour and are invalid for parameters at bounds (flagged). - **Independence assumptions.** Families are fitted and drawn independently (no power↔thermal correlation through shared temperature, none between cross-section regions). Draws within a family are joint. - **Flight update and beam data** are combined as a Bayesian update of the draws; do not additionally scale cross-sections by a flight factor. - **Environment tables are external inputs** and are trusted: flux below the first table point is treated as zero (flagged if it matters); flux above the last point is lumped there. SEP tables must describe the reference event of intensity 1 that the SEP-catalogue intensities are relative to. Trapped-proton SAA time-dependence is not modelled (orbit-averaged tables). - **SEL** is rare: zero-event datasets are refused with a 95 % upper limit (`cross_section_upper_limit`); no Weibull is fitted to them. - **Register/logic AVF, SET pulse width, gate/flop counts, TMR residual, interconnect split** cannot be inferred from these datasets and stay unresolved until `declare()`d. - **Only 2:4 sparsity**, one array dataflow (output-stationary), single dtype per benchmark row. - Fits are single-device: no population/lot variation, no temperature dependence of cross-sections, no beam-angle azimuth effects. --- ## 8. Source code ### `uchu/__init__.py` ```python """Uchu Gijutsu v3 -- space-AI accelerator simulator (single clock, cross-section radiation core).""" from .config import (ComputeConfig, CrossSection, DType, DVFSConfig, MemoryConfig, OrbitalConfig,                      OrbitType, PowerConfig, ProcessNode, RadiationConfig, SimConfig, SolarActivity,                      ThermalConfig) from .faults import flip_bit, measure_gemm_vulnerability from .memory import (WorkloadLayer, build_transformer_workload, estimate_layer, profile_pass,                      structured_sparsity_speedup) from .provenance import MODEL_VERSION, provenance from .radiation import (EccMemory, RadiationEnvironment, RadiationStats, RadiationSubsystem,                         TruncatedPowerLaw, integral_rate) from .rng import child_seed, make_rng from .simulator import ChipSimulator, run_ensemble, run_mission, run_workload from .thermal import LumpedThermalModel, PowerModel __version__ = MODEL_VERSION ``` ### `uchu/rng.py` ```python """Deterministic, isolated random-number streams. v3 never touches NumPy's / Python's / Torch's *global* RNG state.  Every stochastic component asks for its own named stream derived from one master seed via ``numpy.random.SeedSequence`` (``spawn_key`` = CRC32 of each key). Streams with different keys are statistically independent; identical (seed, keys) always reproduce identical streams, in any process. """ from __future__ import annotations import zlib import numpy as np def _spawn_key(keys) -> tuple:     return tuple(zlib.crc32(str(k).encode("utf-8")) for k in keys) def make_rng(seed: int, *keys) -> np.random.Generator:     """Independent Generator for (seed, *keys)."""     ss = np.random.SeedSequence(entropy=int(seed), spawn_key=_spawn_key(keys))     return np.random.default_rng(ss) def child_seed(seed: int, *keys) -> int:     """Integer seed derived from (seed, *keys); safe to ship to worker processes."""     ss = np.random.SeedSequence(entropy=int(seed), spawn_key=_spawn_key(keys))     return int(ss.generate_state(1, dtype=np.uint64)[0] % (2**63 - 1)) ``` ### `uchu/stats.py` ```python """Small statistics helpers (exact Poisson intervals, Wilson, bootstrap).""" from __future__ import annotations import math from typing import Optional, Tuple import numpy as np from scipy.stats import chi2, norm def poisson_rate_ci(k: int, exposure: float, conf: float = 0.95) -> Tuple[float, float]:     """Exact (Garwood) two-sided CI for a Poisson rate = k / exposure."""     if exposure <= 0:         return (0.0, math.inf)     alpha = 1.0 - conf     lo = 0.0 if k == 0 else 0.5 * chi2.ppf(alpha / 2.0, 2 * k)     hi = 0.5 * chi2.ppf(1.0 - alpha / 2.0, 2 * (k + 1))     return lo / exposure, hi / exposure def wilson_ci(successes: int, n: int, conf: float = 0.95) -> Tuple[float, float]:     """Wilson score interval for a binomial proportion."""     if n <= 0:         return (0.0, 1.0)     z = norm.ppf(0.5 + conf / 2.0)     p = successes / n     denom = 1.0 + z * z / n     centre = (p + z * z / (2 * n)) / denom     half = z * math.sqrt(p * (1 - p) / n + z * z / (4 * n * n)) / denom     return max(0.0, centre - half), min(1.0, centre + half) def bootstrap_ci(data, n_boot: int = 2000, conf: float = 0.95,                  rng: Optional[np.random.Generator] = None) -> Tuple[float, float]:     """Percentile bootstrap CI for the mean (vectorised)."""     data = np.asarray(data, float)     rng = rng or np.random.default_rng(0)     idx = rng.integers(0, len(data), size=(n_boot, len(data)))     means = data[idx].mean(axis=1)     a = (1 - conf) / 2     return float(np.quantile(means, a)), float(np.quantile(means, 1 - a)) ``` ### `uchu/config.py` ```python """Configuration for Uchu Gijutsu v3.
